ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e3106	Mr. Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XXI/M/M_00037/index.shtml -- 37/M]] What are your objectives in the ongoing financial compensatory negotiations?
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e3134	Please answer me, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e3149	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen. I can answer the question as follows: In addition to the area of financial compensation in the narrower sense, in view of the time-limited arrangements provided for in the Financial Balance Act in 1997, it is also possible to discuss the financing of hospitals and the promotion of housing. On the occasion of the first two rounds of negotiations, the financial compensatory partners also agreed on a catalogue of questions to be discussed during the financial compensatory negotiations. Experience gained from the implementation of the financial compensation laws has shown that there have always been difficulties in those areas where there has been overlaps in the area of responsibility of the local authorities. Typical example known to all of us: Land teacher. Therefore, one of the most urgent objectives of the negotiations is to combine responsibility for expenditure with responsibility for expenditure. Other general government objective must certainly be to provide the basis for the reorganisation of the government budget even in the financial compensatory negotiations. I've always stressed: If one tries to make savings, correct the state budget, then not only the federal government is required, but all the local authorities, including countries, cities and municipalities, are required. And that is why the contribution of 0.5 percent of the gross domestic product will be mandatory and another consolidation contribution by the countries, cities and communities will be the goal of my person in the financial compensatory negotiations.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e4286	Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XXI/M/M_00029/index.shtml -- 29/M]] What ensures the depoliticisation of the new Supervisory Board of ÖIAG?
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e4313	Please answer me, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e4340	Ladies and gentlemen! In accordance with the ÖIAG Act 2000, which was adopted here in the House, the depoliticisation of the Supervisory Board of ÖIAG is ensured by a new order mechanism insofar as future orders by the Supervisory Board itself have to be made after a first order on a proposal from the Federal Government. We took part in the general meeting of the ÖIAG on 17 February. ==References====External links== In addition to five Supervisory Board members nominated by the Federal Workers' Chamber, they belong to ten members who have been appointed by the capital side. These are entrepreneurial personalities, which total about 3,000 billion Schilling sales in their companies – more than the Austrian gross domestic product –. These are entrepreneurs who stand together for about 650 000 employees and thus have simply shown in their respective companies, in their private careers and careers that they understand how they understand how to create value, and are also able to exercise very exemplary responsibility for employees. In this respect, the order mechanism, as provided for in the law, and on the other hand, a voluntary commitment to objectively target supervisory councils over a headhunt, were designed to achieve a real depoliticisation of the Supervisory Board.

The Federal Ministry of Finance has carried out a procedure corresponding to ÖNORM A 2050 for the collection of offers from personnel consulting companies. Following the examination of all the tenders received, Egon Zehnder International was qualified as the best bidder in terms of the specific tasks in terms of technical and economic terms and was commissioned to carry out a professional and objective procedure to draw up a corresponding selection list of appropriate supervisory candidates. In a first proposal we received about 40 candidates selected at international level. When I think that a Professor Hubbert, Member of the Board of DaimlerChrysler, or a Dr. Achleitner, Chief Financial Officer of Allianz, has managed to get to Austria in Supervisory Boards, we can rightly welcome the fact that something has never been achieved in the history of the state-owned industry and my knowledge has not yet been achieved in any other Supervisory Board in Austria, and that we have here a Supervisory Board which is over all doubts and thus will also guarantee that the achievement of the goal of the Federal Government, our common goal – namely privatization in the interests of the Austrian economy, the industrial location and the employees as decided to move forward and implement – will actually succeed.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e4784	Federal Minister! In terms of fairer financial compensation, the old coalition is known to have failed. ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e4802	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e4818	Ladies and gentlemen! In the Financial Constitution Act 1948, which forms the constitutional basis for each financial compensation law, the principle of balanced burden distribution is laid down. This principle essentially states that the financial equalisation schemes must be carried out in accordance with the capacity of the local authorities involved. Based on this basic requirement for each financial compensation, the task of meeting the larger communities in relation to smaller towns will be quite different, on the one hand, and on the other hand, I give you the right to re-examine historically developed distributions of finances, public charges and, on the other hand, to reach a new agreement in a consistent manner. However, I should also like to point out that in the past there have always been changes in the financial compensatory negotiations. For example, in the 1985 financial compensatory law, the lowest doubling for municipalities with a maximum population of 1,000 has been eliminated, thus making these small communities equal to those with a population of 1 001 to 10 000. This led to a significant financial improvement compared with the old scheme. In 1993 the Financial Balance Act introduced an annual base amount of 102,3 S per capita, which also meant a financial improvement for the small municipalities at the expense of the large municipalities. These rules of the past, through which it was possible to take first steps to strengthen the financial capacity of the smaller communities, could therefore, if the will exists among all partners, continue in the future and come to a redefinition here.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e9275	Mr President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) With today's Social Security Amendment Act, two topics are on the agenda, namely the democratisation of the social security bodies' bodies and, on the other hand, the necessary response of policy to the ECJ judgment of 23. May this year. On both issues, this government, this coalition, on the one hand, proves its strong awareness of democracy, but on the other, its high sense of responsibility especially for the socially weak and its high sense of responsibility towards the taxpayer. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Social security is a particularly important institution for ensuring social security and the solidarity of all citizens in the event of illness, accident and retirement. It must be an important policy concern to carry out meaningful reforms here. The Social Insurance Amendment Act provides for a democratisation in the composition of the social security bodies and is therefore a long-standing step that abolishes an unsustainable situation, which has so far existed, namely that the presidents of the interest groups have arbitrarily composed these bodies at their own discretion. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] This can no longer happen in the future, because in future the outcome of the democratic elections of these representations is to be taken into account according to the d’Hondt system. That is to say, it is an important step towards democratization. It is also particularly important – this is a sign that we are actually in favour of this democratization – that certain carriers and the power of their structures must be assessed differently, such as the social insurance institution of the railways, such as the operating funds. One was sensitive enough and has also taken into account this particular situation in the legal regulation. What is important to me is also a new situation of non-actives in the social security bodies. These are the pensioners, and these are the disabled. They have so far had relatively weak rights in these bodies, in the advisory committees, and they have now been strengthened by not being able to match them in these bodies and by being able to submit their own requests, their concerns on the Board of Directors and the General Assembly. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] In my opinion, this is an important step towards more rights for the elderly and especially for the disabled. I see this step in connection with the installation of the Senior Advisory Council in the Federal Ministry for Social Security and Generations as an important step towards the living design of the Generational Treaty. Ladies and gentlemen! We do not have to downgrad the generation contract to a lip commitment, but we actually have to live it, and we have to put credible measures in politics, in the legal regulations to show that it is serious to us. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] On the second topic, in response to the ECJ judgment of 23. May this year. I would like to say a few words about pension reform in general, because it has been mentioned very often today. We are concerned with ensuring that pensions are financed in the long term. Ladies and gentlemen! This is an issue that plays an increasingly important role in the EU as well. Austria has also received a recommendation from the EU to adapt and reorganise its pension system and to take into account the enormous demographic change that is taking place at the moment. I would like to stress that this pension reform, which we will shortly decide on, with very high sensitivity, was designed with very great social competence, that the necessary measures are significant and that they would have been due in the last legislative period. – In order to rebut the argument that this pension reform is being implemented quickly and insurmountably. The reforms we are proposing today should have taken place in the last legislative period, and therefore there can be no mention of "rasch" and "convention". [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] If we are talking about "grievances", I would like to say that this pension reform does not contain any horrors at all, and there will be no horrors in the future. [[Abg. Oillingers: You said that yourself!]] [[Abg. Ollinger: That's your quote!]] It is about making it clear that we do not allow any social cold to take place, but rather that this pension reform is aimed at reducing social security by strengthening the social network by ensuring long-term financial stability and ensuring security for the socially weak. [[Abg. Oellinger: You said that yourself, Mrs. Minister!]] Maybe short to the history: A new early retirement pension was introduced in 1993. Men and women could retire at 55 years of age if they were no longer operational in their then activities due to certain physical problems. This was an exception to the system, and this exception has been created only because one wanted to solve employment problems because one wanted to get the financial situation in certain large companies and did not send people into unemployment, but into retirement – at the expense of the taxpayer and at the expense of this system. In 1996, the age of entry for men was increased to 57 years, and this is not the case in the EU. Therefore, this scheme was adopted by the EU at 23. In May of this year. This means that, according to this ECJ knowledge, men with 55 years of age could also retire. We've seen explosion-like requests from men of 50 years of age have been made. Ladies and gentlemen! This means an inviolable burden, a disastrous impact on the budget. The experts have calculated that this would mean 1 to 2 billion schilling a year. There is also no argument of protection of trust, because these men, who now suddenly wanted to retire because of the ECJ judgment at 55 years of age, could not rely before that the ECJ judgment would be such a failure. Therefore, it is unfair and unfair to the others that these people can now suddenly retire. As I said, the impact on taxpayers is simply not responsible. This is an extraordinary improvement for the unearned, the farmers and the tradesmen, for they have not had this professional protection that they now have in the past. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] I should like to stress that this step has been taken again by something that has long been due, namely professional protection for the unearned, and these are the socially weak. If you, ladies and gentlemen of the opposition, accuse us that we are not socially competent, you confirm the opposite. It is precisely with this regulation that we have realized something you would have had to do for a long time if you had actually wanted to stand up for the unearned, for the small earners, for the farmers, for the commercialists, for the small and medium-sized enterprises. The efforts of the volunteer fire brigades, the professional firearms and the operational fire brigades have shifted over the last decades from the classical fire fighting to more technical operations. In recent years, the fire brigade has been more than 200 000 times a year, and of these operations, about two thirds are technical operations and only one third are used to combat fire. Technical assistance includes, for example, the recovery of accident victims from car wrecks. In the case of such operations, the fire brigade shall be responsible for the release of injured persons from an emergency situation, the recovery, the initial supply of the injured and the subsequent transfer to the rescue organizations. There is also the risk of cut-offs in recoverys from broken cars, and it is therefore necessary to include fire brigades members in the scope of the occupational disease list. I would ask those Members who vote in favour of the bill in third reading, 'Yes', those who vote against, 'No' vote in the mood. I now ask the writer, Mrs Reitsamer, to begin the call for name. Mrs Haller will replace them later.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e14583	Mr President. Federal Minister! Allow us all to change the subject. I would like to refer to the Government's draft agreement on the minimum age for admission to employment. First of all, I would like to deal with a decision of the June 1997 General Conference of the International Labour Organisation, whose content we want to ratify today and which we hope will also be. It is essentially an amendment to the Constitution of the International Labour Organisation, which is referred to as the instrument of amendment to the Constitution of the International Labour Organisation in 1997. You know that there is currently no way for the International Labour Conference to abolish accepted agreements that have become inconsistencies or no longer make a useful contribution to achieving the Organization's objectives. This unclear state is to be abolished with ratification and thereby restored legal certainty. Just as in this area, I think, we should also do so in the area of the complete abolition of child labour by also ratifying the International Convention on the Minimum Age of Employment Authorisation today. This Convention No. 138 also belongs to the seven core standards of the International Labour Organisation. I can only recall Article 1 once again, which obliges each Member State to pursue a national policy designed to ensure the effective abolition of child labour and to increase the minimum age for admission to employment or work to a level where the full physical and mental development of young people is ensured, namely, 15 years. With regard to age, however, there are very few exceptions. In the meantime, almost all the obstacles to ratification have been removed from us, most recently – you remember – with the amendment to the 1998 Agricultural and Forestry Employment Act. That is why this ratification is correct and important. I should like to mention a need for adaptation. In the legislative text, it says: "The competent authority shall determine the activities in which... employment or work may be authorised." – That is, in the Child and Youth Employment Act, it is very well regulated how these easy activities look, but still not in the Land Labor Act. There, I think there is still a need for adaptation. On the Land Labor Act, when I am already on the subject, it is to be said that we welcome the amendment to the Land Labor Act in principle, as it provides for adjustments in the field of worker protection. In our opinion, however, there are large deficits in the area of apprentices, where we believe that adjustments are necessary, because the basic question is: When's an apprenticeship finished? It is now true that apprentices in agriculture and forestry as well as in commercial apprentices can end their apprenticeship early, up to the earliest ten weeks before the end of the teaching period. It is true, however, that in the field of agriculture and forestry, teachers must agree, despite existing examinations, that the teaching relationship ends in advance. For us, this means an inequality between the apprentices in agriculture and forestry compared to the apprentices covered by the Vocational Training Act. We have tabled an amendment to the Committee in order to amend the Land Arbeitsgesetz in such a way that, so to speak, the teaching relationship in the event of the transfer of the skilled worker's examination before the end of the teaching period automatically ends at the end of the week in which the examination is completed. This was rejected by the blue-black majority. Mr Donabauer said that there were some apprentices with him who didn't want to. Of course, we have also talked to apprentices, of whom we have heard the opposite. I can't imagine apprentices coming and saying: Please, please, don't change the law, I would like to continue my apprenticeship content and abandon my company content! We can't imagine that. I personally find this non-equilibrium unfair – to use the word "unfair" here as well. However, we will agree to the changes in the Land Labor Act.

[[Abg. Dr. Mertel: From you!]] With this new law, we are also saying quite clearly, in the direction of the Chamber of Workers: The carnival's out! There is now a reform which we promised people before the election! [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We implement this by adopting an AK law novel, which is a really decisive step towards justice, towards the reduction of privileges, but also towards transparency and control at the Chambers of Workers. At the same time – and this is the important thing! – we also create the urgently necessary legal adaptation to the Federal Remuneration Act, which has not yet been implemented, which you have forgotten in the decision of the Federal Remuneration Act, where there has been no legal certainty. That is why this is an important step as far as the Chambers themselves are concerned. [[Abg. Dietachmayr: We have done this for a long time!]] I put it to you on six points, Mrs Reitsamer, because you said nothing would change or we would not change anything. Six points are essential. First point: It will no longer depend on what the AK Board of Directors imposes on functional fees in the future, but, in the future, functional fees will be fixed by the General Assembly of the Chamber of Workers, provided that there are any decisions to be made. So far, as has been said, the AK Board of Directors has done so. In addition, approval by the supervisory authority is required, in this case by the Federal Ministry. This is a good scheme that not only provides comprehensive information on the level of individual functional fees, but also creates more transparency and more control. Second point: We have ensured that workers' chamber presidents – mainly in the discussion – can no longer receive an AK-pension in the future. There is always the objection that this has already been regulated by the Federal Remuneration Act. That is true, but it was still in the obsolete Chamber of Workers' Law, which is now being reformed. We had to fix that. The same applies to the third point, to the discharges. It will no longer exist in the future. Fourth point: It is also decisive that in future officials of the Chamber of Labour may no longer be paid flat-rate compensation. There will be no longer a flat-rate cost replacement. That was a center, a hort of abuse, and we have corrected and abolished that with this new law. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] The fifth point – this is a very important point, a point that has created a special degree of scrutinity in the current legal situation – concerns the question of service contracts for workers' chamber presidents. You have to imagine this: So far, workers' chamber presidents have negotiated a service contract with the Board of Directors itself, which has essentially remained hidden somewhere. That is to say, an AK president has actually negotiated service contracts with himself, which have always been the subject of wide-ranging discussions in the media, because they have contained exactly those privileges that we have criticized for years. That won't happen in the future. In the future, contracts will be subject to strict control, and they can no longer be made with the AK Board because there will be no more. There will be no more service contracts that will make possible all these things that we have always criticized, such as additional credits, service cars, service homes and so on. This will no longer be because we are now adopting a corresponding legislative amendment. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] [[Abg. Dietachmayr: There are no service apartments!]] [[Abg. Dr. Mertel: Tell us who has a service apartment! Blow soaps!]] In the future, therefore, it is excluded that AK Presidents receive further benefits in addition to the functional fees that will be subject to strict control. They receive, where appropriate, functional fees, and also this not fourteen times, but only twelve times, and in turn the supervisory authority, that is, the Federal Ministry, has to pay attention to compliance with these provisions. Sixth point: Not only the supervisory authority's rights are extended, but also the supervisory powers of the Court of Auditors. Ladies and gentlemen! We have here an AK reform, an AK novelle, which is actually an essential step forward and the end is with unjustified privileges and unlawful salary payments. ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e19835	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! One thing can certainly not be discussed away: We are moving towards an ageing society in which more and more older people will face fewer and less younger people. This is certainly the biggest social policy challenge that we will have to face in the near future, a challenge to society, to the institutions and also to the state. If one assumes that the number of young people decrease by 20 percent in equal numbers within 30 years, the number of adults decreases by 17 percent up to 59 years, but the proportion of over 60-year-olds increases by 67 percent, I think one can understand the size of the challenge. A first step has already been taken in Austria in the form of the Federal-Senioren Act 1998. We also voted in favour of this, even though we were in opposition at the time. The advice, information and care of the seniors was thus secured on the basis of a law, also financially secured. About 21 million Schillings are currently available – distributed by the Federal Chancellery. If today a request of the opposition is made to relocate this promotion to the Federal Chancellery, I have to say that you just want to turn the wheel back. We prefer to turn the wheel forward and move all these agendas to the Federal Ministry of Social Security and Generations, where they are certainly best located. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The second goal of the Federal-Seniors Act 1998 was the Federal-Seniors' Advisory Council, which had also been established at the Federal Chancellor's Office. It has proved that the chief thing that Mrs. Reitsamer spoke about was not really a matter of chief interest to the elderly. The Federal-Senior Advisory Council was all this time called up by the Federal Chancellor only once. That means he was simply ignored. Of course, we don't want that anymore in the future. It is therefore new to this novel that the Federal Senior Advisory Council should now be established where it belongs – in this new ministry – and that it should become an active governance instrument for senior policy, but not a mere representation instrument. It is also new that the so-called "Austrian Seniors' Council" should be used in the form of an association of the umbrella association of the Seniors' Organisations, which should be more than a strong voice for the interests of the older people, namely a legal representation of interests, which from our point of view could be referred to as the third chamber and as the representative of interests of older people – in addition to the servants and the economic operators. As a result, the senior representatives are no longer sitting at the door, but they are sitting at the table in negotiations that concern their interests. You can decide in concrete terms. They have a seat and a vote when it comes to senior pension policy, for example, pension reform or social security schemes. They may make applications and submit opinions. In this way, Austria is really an example within the framework of the EU. Austria is the only country within the EU that will have this law in place in the future. I would like to congratulate you very warmly, Mrs Federal Minister.

For this reason, our request concerning the amendment of the Federal-Senior Law is also very welcome. We thus contribute efficiently to the creation of the legal framework, which does not at all make the key to the conflict of generations a real danger to our society. With the present application for the Federal-Senioren Act, the Austrian Seniors Council is legally recognized and upgraded. I would like to address a point which the SPÖ and also the Greens have brought back and forced very much. This concerns the redeployment of seniors now to the Federal Ministry of Social Security and Generations. To this end, I would like to say: This was a long wish both by Minister Bartenstein and by those responsible in the Austrian federal states. This is also true in other EU countries, and I think it is a good signal to have the youth and the older generation in a ministry. We know that we still need to improve a lot for this age group. Therefore, we want to be direct contact persons and an honest platform for the elderly in Austria. It must not be too old to be burdened. We can make a significant contribution to this through the guarantee of participation and co-decision of the seniors in Austria. I'm building on the exchange between young and older.

Point Report of the Economic and Social Committee on the Government Template (97 of the Supplements): Federal Law amending the Federal Law on the Award of Prizes (Preisausgesetz – PrAG) and the Federal Law against Unfair Competition 1984 (148 of the Supplements)
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e21226	We're on the 10th. Agenda item. The first mention is made by Mr Kurt Eder. The speaking time is set at 7 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e21253	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Let me briefly make a few remarks on the award-winning law and the amendment we are discussing here today. Indeed, it is becoming increasingly difficult for consumers to compare prices, because the packaging units, which are compacted in the packaging, are different from what the consumer often thinks. There is therefore a European directive which is now to be implemented with this Novelle Awarding Act. However, there are some points in this implementation that I am not happy with and why my group may not be able to vote in favour of either. In general, there is no harmonisation of the pricing law. This means that consumers will have problems in this area even after this innovation. Unfortunately, this government template has been rejected, the price awarding rules in Austria, which are rather fragmented, such as for banks, for driving schools, for exchange offices – and then Mr Maier will go a little closer – as well as for telecommunications and so on, in order to mention some examples, to sum up in a matter. This year's wish of consumer protection facilities is not in line with this, and unfortunately it remains in this fragmentation. Another point we are not going to agree with is that there are too many exceptions in this draft. The EU Directive requires a comprehensive indication of the basic price. That is to say, in addition to the selling price of a pack with a certain filling content, about 350 g, the basic price is also to indicate the price of a kilogram of the product or of a unit quantity of the product. In our opinion, this requirement is very inadequate in the government's view. Packs of less than 20 grams or 20 millilitres are excluded at all. And the ever-increasing range of ready-to-eat, dietetic food and concentrates is also excluded. We don't think that's a good solution. It would also have been nice if we already knew today what the regulations, which are then drawn up by this law, will look like, because the Minister of State has a number of possibilities under these regulations. I think it would have been good if we already knew today what these regulations will look like in order to give consumers appropriate safety. An extreme exception for so-called small businesses is also included in this law. However, this derogation for small businesses, which is made possible by the underlying EU Directive, was interpreted in an exorbitant manner in the government template. Thus, shops with less than nine full-time employees are not at all the same as shops whose sales space is less than 250 square meters. That is, everything that moves under 250 square meters within business areas is not subject to this award-winning law-novelle. These derogations, which are still very complicated, are, in our opinion, overshadowed and do not, in my opinion, comply with the EU principle. We cannot therefore give our approval to this amendment. Thank you.

The necessary changes are the consequences of the Labour Rights Change Act 1997, which enabled the Federal Chamber of Architects and Engineering Consuls to opt out of the Commercial Social Insurance Act in pension insurance. The only compulsory pension insurance now is the pension fund of civil engineering chamber members. In order to secure this opt-out solution retroactively, this novelle is necessary. Furthermore, it will be possible by law to provide health care facilities so that the opt-out in health insurance also retroactively with 1. January 2000 will also be safeguarded by law. The new retirement age for women and men is 65. ==References== The precise definition of the survivors seems necessary for civil technicians, since the circle of those entitled was greater than in social law. Capital gains are newly defined as income from welfare institutions. This means that it is necessary to ensure the system of capital coverage in accordance with the law. The new posting provision states that in future 500 members of the board will be sent to the board of trustees, which then manages the whole. However, due to the enlargement of the EU, the principle of unanimity is not only massively threatened, but even shortly before the case. In the past, Spain has already submitted an application to the European Council of Ministers aimed at managing European water resources centrally from Brussels. The countries of the dry and hot south have long been speculating about getting something off the Alpine sources for irrigated agriculture. In order to accommodate new members, one of the key points of the forthcoming EU reform conference will be to limit those areas where unanimity is necessary. However, in order to protect our drinking water from EU access and sales tomorrow, the responsible politicians must act already today. In terms of water quality, we do not need to adapt to the EU. The EU should adapt to us!

ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e25429	The next speaker was Mr Ing. Fallent. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e25451	Mr President. Federal Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! That is what distinguishes us: a balanced and feasible policy. We operate it, and it brings success. And balanced and feasible laws are the ones that will have the future! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] The orientation of what Austria serves is at our heart. If Mr Wimmer says you're on the consumer side, we'll tell you: We are on the side of all Austrians! In this sense, we have also proposed and designed this law by trying, in the course of the weighing of interests, to put all the concerns in it. [[Abg. You can't serve all the gentlemen!]] Yes, I know, but you should at least serve the majority. The Biocidal Product Act is nothing more than the implementation of an EU Directive, which was adopted on 14 November. It was decided in May 1998 and entered into force. Member States now have two years to transform this EU directive into national law. The biocidal Directive aims to regulate the placing on the market of these biocidal products across the EU. The focus is on simplification and harmonisation in the Member States, because there are currently very different rules. The aim is to ensure and ensure a high level of protection for people and the environment. The extent to which European harmonisation is achieved depends decisively on whether Member States apply this Directive in a similar way and whether the authorities apply it in the same way. The aim of this biocidal product law is to create the prerequisite for the placing on the market of biocidal products only if they do not have a harmful impact on human and animal health or an unacceptable impact on the environment when they are intended to be applied. What are biocidal products? – You can say this very briefly: It is ultimately all non-agricultural pesticides. This very broad definition is concrete in a list of 23 product types. During approval and registration, one distinguishes between low risk biocidal products and those with higher risk potential. Those with low risk potential only need to be registered, and those with higher need to be duly approved. There are sufficient transitional periods that you do not want to see. But this is also one thing that ultimately comes to the consumer's attention, because it allows price levels to be kept. Nor can it be that prices rise and therefore consumers have disadvantages. The Federal Government has endeavoured to present a law, in accordance with European directives, which allows for harmonisation in the Member States, in order to ensure the best possible protection of human and animal health and the environment. With this decision-making, Austria will be a model for many other European Member States. Thank you.

Of particular importance to us Austrians is the Convention on the Security of United Nations Staff in the case of peacekeeping operations. The increasing attacks on UN blue helmets in recent times give rise to serious concern and require increased protection of this staff. The figures speak for themselves. Since 1960, 33 Austrian soldiers have been killed in peacekeeping. The murder of two Austrian UN soldiers was particularly spectacular. May 1997 in the Middle East – a criminal case that has not been resolved until today. Currently there are Austrian quotas in Cyprus and on the Golan. So we have every reason to do everything that increases the protection of our countrymen. The agreement, which is truly to be welcomed, lays down, among other things, the obligation to prosecute criminal offences committed against United Nations personnel and the obligation to extradite the perpetrators. Today, the National Council will also agree to a second agreement to bring Switzerland closer to the European Union. The corresponding agreement concerns the free movement of persons and is part of a package of contracts consisting of seven. As you can see, the Swiss people and their government continue to prefer bilateral treaties to ensure their legitimate interests to a rapid accession to the EU. It is interesting that the agreement contains a transitional mechanism for which there is no parallel in any other Community agreement. In principle, full freedom of movement of persons is provided for after five years from the date of entry into force. However, Switzerland can once again introduce quotas for two years – I emphasize: unilaterally – if there is a strong immigration. It can be seen that Switzerland has consistently expressed its position and has been able to reach special arrangements for its benefit. I believe that this could be an example for Austria if I think in this context of the forthcoming enlargement of the EU. The third and last point I would like to mention is a Treaty which was not discussed in the Committee on Foreign Affairs. It is the Association Agreement between the European Union and the Hashemite Kingdom of Jordan to be ratified by Austria. Similar contracts already exist with the other Mediterranean countries Turkey, Malta, Cyprus, Israel, Tunisia and Morocco. The aim of these agreements is to create a framework for bilateral political dialogue and to promote the expansion of trade and investment. Cooperation on economic, social, cultural and financial matters should be strengthened. I think it is in the interest of Austria to contribute to stability in the Middle East. The Freedom Party will therefore agree to all these contracts.

However, for the reasons I have mentioned – unlimited, no cover for the gift tax – we reject the second bill. - Thank you.

Since then, the tax root has actually fallen away, but for many years still there has been a very strong sympathy for our savers as regards anonymity. On the other hand, we can see that the increased educational work of the monetary institutions and the trend towards higher forms of savings have recently made the issue of anonymity relatively difficult. Nevertheless, we have not made it easy. Mr Van der Bellen! In the end, that was the opinion of FATF, the OECD Financial Action Task Force, which, as you have rightly said, was what we meant in order to avert damage to our country: We must try to find a balance between maintaining an incredibly high savings family in Austria on the one hand and the international fight against money laundering on the other. ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e29369	The next speaker was Mr Müller. Voluntary speaking time limit: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00030_d7e29390	Thank you very much, Mr President. Thank you very much, Mr State Secretary! High house! As my previous speakers have pointed out, there has been an OECD call for Austria to amend the anonymous savings book since 1989. In 1995, the EU Commission filed a complaint and, as expected, the ECJ's decision on the disfavor of Austria failed. The improvements now in place were necessary because the OECD FATF working group made additional claims to combat money laundering. What does the government template look like? – From November of this year, anonymous savings books may no longer be opened. In the case of existing savings books, the customer must legitimise with the bank from November 2000. However, deposits and transfers may only be made to identified accounts from November 2000. Fixes are up to 30. ==References====External links== Should be up to 30. In the case of savings books with a balance of at least 200 000 S, payment is only possible after waiting for a seven-day period in June 2002. In that period, the Ministry of the Interior shall, after a corresponding report by the Bank, examine whether the suspicion is based on money laundering. If the savings account is not on the name of the customer and has a credit of less than 200 000 S, the savings account may continue to be cancelled under the designation of the agreed ransom word. Except for a unique identification, these savings books do not change anything for the savers. All these measures would have to be sufficient to close even the last money laundering loops that were feared by FATF. With the abolition of the anonymity of savings deposits, the current Federal Government is taking the long-standing step of complying with OECD regulations. With the gift tax, all Austrians gain the opportunity not to pay a tax. We've heard a lot about winners and losers today. In this innovation by the Federal Government there are only winners.
